\begin{split}&\left\langle{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle=\left\langle{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*},{\mathcal{A}}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}),{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle=\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{2}^{2}\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-A-2}}{\geq}\frac{4}{5}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(D.12)

Next we give an upper bound of
\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|
. To this end, we need to use the fact that
{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}
is a summation of rank-
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
tensors. By Lemma
E.7
, for any rank-
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
tensor
{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
, we have

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}),{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-A-2}}{\leq}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{3}{2}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

Thus by plugging each component in the right hand side of (
D.10
) in
{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
and using triangle inequality, we obtain the following upper bound:

\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}C_{\Delta}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F},

where
C_{\Delta}
is defined as

\begin{split}&C_{\Delta}:=\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|\mathcal{S}^{*}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

By the proof of Lemma
E.3
, we know that

\begin{split}C_{\Delta}&\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-asmp-1}}{\leq}1.01\overline{\lambda}b^{-3}(E^{(t)})^{3/2}+3(1.01b)^{2}E^{(t)}+3\cdot\frac{1.01\overline{\lambda}}{b^{3}}\cdot(1.01b)E^{(t)}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-asmp-2}}{\leq}&\left(1.01c_{0}^{1/2}\overline{\lambda}\underline{\lambda}^{1/4}b^{-3}+6\cdot 1.01^{2}b^{2}\right)E^{(t)}\\
\leq&6.5\kappa^{1/2}\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}E^{(t)}.\\
\end{split}

Note that the above inequality is similar to (
C.16
) in the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
Combining all above, we obtain:

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\leq\frac{3}{2}C_{\Delta}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
\leq&10\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\cdot\left(\kappa^{1/2}\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}E^{(t)}\right)\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+100\kappa\underline{\lambda}(E^{(t)})^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-asmp-2}}{\leq}&\frac{1}{4}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{100c_{0}\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}.\end{split}

(D.13)

In the meantime, by the same argument in (
C.17
) in the proof of Theorem
3.1
, we can show that

\left|\left\langle 4{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{(t)}-\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{X}}_{k}^{(t)},\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\rangle\right|\leq c_{1}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}+\frac{81\kappa^{2}}{4c_{1}}\xi^{2},

(D.14)

and
c_{1}
is some universal constant which will be specified later. Now
combining (
D.11
), (
D.12
), (
D.13
) and (
D.14
) and specifying
a=\frac{2b^{4}}{3\kappa^{2}}
(which is similar to (
C.20
)), we obtain that

\begin{split}&Q_{\mathcal{S},1}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}Q_{k,1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\kappa^{-2}b^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\\
&\qquad+\frac{a}{8}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\frac{c_{0}\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{6\kappa^{1/2}}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{20}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\frac{(100c_{0}+c_{1})\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}-C_{1}\kappa^{2}\xi^{2}\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-equivalent-criteria}}}{\geq}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{960\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}+\frac{a}{8}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{1}{20}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\frac{(100c_{0}+c_{2})\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}-C_{1}\kappa^{2}\xi^{2}\\
&=\rho\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{3/2}}{\kappa^{1/2}}E^{(t)}+\frac{a}{8}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{1}{20}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-C_{1}\kappa^{2}\xi^{2},\end{split}

Here in the last step, we specify
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
to be small constants such that
\rho:=\frac{1}{960}-100c_{0}-c_{1}>0
, and
C_{1}=\frac{81}{4c_{1}}
, which gives (
D.9
).

Step 4

In this step, we provide the error contraction of
E^{(t)}
. By plugging in
a=\frac{2b^{4}}{3\kappa^{2}}
, we have

\begin{split}&Q_{\mathcal{S},2}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}Q_{k,2}\\
&\leq 12b^{6}\left\|\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\frac{2b^{10}}{\kappa^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+12b^{6}\xi^{2},\end{split}

and

\left\|\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)})-\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\left\|{\mathcal{A}}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-A-2}}{\leq}\frac{16}{9}\left(\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{n}\vee 1\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

Now by taking
\eta=\eta_{0}/b^{6}
in (
D.8
) and applying (
D.9
), we have

\begin{split}E^{(t+1)}\leq&\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(t)}-\left(\frac{1}{10b^{6}}\eta_{0}-\frac{64}{3b^{6}}\left(\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{n}\vee 1\right)\eta_{0}^{2}\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&-\left(\frac{b^{-2}}{12\kappa^{2}}\eta_{0}-\frac{2b^{-2}}{\kappa^{4}}\eta_{0}^{2}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(t)}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2C_{1}b^{-6}\kappa^{2}\eta_{0}\xi^{2}+12b^{-6}\eta_{0}^{2}\xi^{2}.\end{split}

(D.15)

Then as long as
\eta_{0}<\frac{3}{640}\left(\frac{n}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\wedge 1\right)
,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{10b^{6}}\eta_{0}-\frac{64}{3b^{6}}\left(\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{n}\vee 1\right)\eta_{0}^{2}>0,\\
&\frac{b^{-2}}{12\kappa^{2}}\eta_{0}-\frac{2b^{-2}}{\kappa^{4}}\eta_{0}^{2}>0,\end{split}

and it follows that

\begin{split}&E^{(t+1)}\leq\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(t)}+(2C_{1}+1)\kappa^{2}b^{-6}\eta_{0}\xi^{2}.\end{split}

(D.16)

By induction (as we proved for (
C.24
)), we can then show that

\begin{split}E^{(t)}&\leq\frac{(2C_{1}+1)\kappa^{4}\xi^{2}}{2\rho b^{6}}+\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)^{t}E^{(0)}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq-regression-A-1}}{\leq}C\kappa^{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}\cdot df}{nb^{6}}+\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)^{t}E^{(0)}.\end{split}

(D.17)

Applying Lemma
E.2
, we then have

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 42b^{6}E^{(t)}\leq C\kappa^{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}\cdot df}{n}+42\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)^{t}E^{(0)}.\\
&\leq C\left(\kappa^{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}\cdot df}{n}+\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)^{t}\kappa^{2}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Therefore, for sufficiently large
T
, one can see that we have
\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(T)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C\kappa^{4}\frac{\sigma^{2}\cdot df}{n}
.

Step 5

Finally, we check the validity of conditions. We first assume the conditions (
D.6
), (
D.7
) hold, and verify (
D.4
) and (
D.5
); then we show that (
D.6
) and (
D.7
) hold with high probability. We start from (
D.5
). By the proof of
(
Zhang et al. 2019
, Theorem 4)
, we know as long as

n\geq C\frac{\left(\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\right)\left(\overline{p}^{3/2}+\kappa\overline{p}\cdot\overline{r}\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}},

the initialization estimator has the following error bound:

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{df(\sigma^{2}+\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2})}{n}\leq C^{\prime}\frac{df\cdot\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{n},

for some universal constants
C,C^{\prime}
.
Then by Lemma
E.2
, we have

\begin{split}&E^{(0)}\leq 11\kappa^{2}b^{-6}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\frac{C\kappa^{2}df\cdot\overline{r}\overline{\lambda}^{2}}{n\overline{\lambda}^{3/2}}\leq c_{0}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{\kappa^{3/2}}.\end{split}

Here we use the assumption that

n\geq C\kappa^{4}df\cdot\overline{r}.

Thus we show that (
D.5
) holds at
t=0
. Now suppose (
D.5
) holds at
t
, we show it also holds at
t+1
. By (
D.16
),

\begin{split}E^{(t+1)}&\leq\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(t)}+(2C_{1}+1)\kappa^{2}b^{-6}\eta_{0}\xi^{2}\\
&\leq\left(1-\frac{2\rho\eta_{0}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)c_{0}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{\kappa^{3/2}}+(2C_{1}+1)\kappa^{2}b^{-6}\eta_{0}\xi^{2}\\
&=c_{0}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{\kappa^{3/2}}-\left(\frac{2c_{0}\rho\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{\kappa^{3.5}}-(2C_{1}+1)\kappa^{1/2}\underline{\lambda}^{-3/2}\xi^{2}\right)\eta_{0}\\
&\leq c_{0}\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{1/2}}{\kappa^{3/2}}.\end{split}

Here in the last inequality we use the signal-noise-ratio assumption:
\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\kappa^{4}\frac{df\sigma^{2}}{n}
. Thus (
D.5
) is verified for any
t=0,\ldots,t_{max}
. The verification of (
D.4
) is the same as we did for (
C.3
), and we omitted it here.
Now we start to show that (
D.6
), (
D.7
) hold with high probability. Since
n\geq Cdf
, we know that (
D.7
) holds with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-c\cdot df\right)
by Lemma
E.6
.

On the other hand, by definition,

\begin{split}\xi:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\\
{\mathbf{W}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times r_{1}},\left\|{\mathbf{W}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\varepsilon),\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle.\end{split}

Notice that for any fixed
\mathcal{S},W_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}
, one have

\begin{split}&\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\varepsilon),\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle=\left\langle\varepsilon,{\mathcal{A}}\left(\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right)\right\rangle,\end{split}

which has normal distribution with mean zero and variance
\frac{\tau^{2}\sigma^{2}}{n}
when conditional on
\{{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, with
\tau:=\left\|{\mathcal{A}}\left(\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right)\right\|_{2}
. Thus we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\varepsilon),\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle\geq t\right|\{{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)\leq e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\tau^{2}\sigma^{2}}}.

Under the event
A:=\left\{\text{\eqref{eq-regression-A-2} holds}\right\}
,
\tau^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}
, then it follows that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\varepsilon),\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle\geq t\right|A\right)\leq e^{-\frac{nt^{2}}{3\sigma^{2}}}.

(D.18)

Now for
k=1,2,3
, we can construct an
\varepsilon
-net
\left\{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(N_{k})}\right\}
of Stiefel
\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
with metric
d({\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2})=\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}-{\mathbf{V}}_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}\right\|
, such that

\begin{split}\sup_{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}}\min_{i\leq N_{k}}d({\mathbf{V}}_{k},{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(i)})\leq\varepsilon\end{split}

and
N_{k}\leq\left(\frac{c_{0}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p_{k}(p_{k}-r_{k})}
for some absolute constant
c_{0}
.
Also, we can construct
\varepsilon
-net
\{\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{S}^{(N_{\mathcal{S}})}\}
for the core tensors on
\mathbb{B}_{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}}
, which is the
l_{2}
unit ball for
\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}}
, such that

\sup_{\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{B}_{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}}}\min_{i\leq N_{\mathcal{S}}}\left\|\mathcal{S}-\mathcal{S}^{(i)}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\varepsilon

and
N_{\mathcal{S}}\leq\left(\frac{c_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)^{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}.

Then by the similar
\varepsilon-
net argument in Lemma
E.5
, we can show that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\xi>2\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}df}{n}}\right|A\right)\leq C\exp\left(-c\cdot df)\right),\end{split}

and it follows that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\xi<2\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}df}{n}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}\left(A\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(\left.N_{1}>C\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}df}{n}}\right|A\right)\\
&\geq\left(1-Ce^{-c\cdot df}\right)\left(1-Ce^{-c\cdot df}\right)\geq 1-2Ce^{-c\cdot df}.\end{split}

Thus we proved that with probability at least
1-Ce^{-c\cdot df}
, (
D.7
) holds, now the proof is finished.
\square

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4.3

We first introduce some notations to simplify the proof. Recall for each
(j,k,l)\in[p_{1}]\times[p_{2}]\times[p_{3}]
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\sim\text{Poisson}(\nu_{jkl})
, where
\nu_{jkl}:=I\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{jkl})
. By Assumption
4.1
, we have
|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{jkl}|\leq B
. We next define a new random tensor
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
such that
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}={\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}1_{\left\{\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10}\leq{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\leq 10\nu_{jkl}\right\}}+\nu_{jkl}1_{\left\{{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\not\in[\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10},10\nu_{jkl}]\right\}}
, and we further define
{\mathcal{Z}}:={\mathcal{Z}}_{1}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
where

\begin{split}{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}&=\log\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}+1/2\right)-\mathbb{E}\log({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}+1/2),\\
{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}&=\mathbb{E}\log({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}+1/2)-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}-\log I.\end{split}

The following conditions are introduced for the proof:

(
A_{1}
)

{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}={\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl},\quad\forall(j,k,l)\in[p_{1}]\times[p_{2}]\times[p_{3}]

(D.19)

(
A_{2}
)

\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\right\|\leq 2K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}}\right),\quad k=1,2,3

(D.20)

(
A_{3}
)

\sup_{\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I/e^{B}}},

(D.21)

(
A_{4}
)

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\
\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Y}}/I-\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\right\rangle\leq C_{2}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I/e^{B}}}.

(D.22)

Here
K_{0},C_{1},C_{2}
are some universal constants and
df:=r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}
. We now start the analysis assuming that the above conditions hold, and we will verify that they hold with high probability at last.

First of all, by Lemma
E.9
, if we set
I\geq Ce^{B}
, then for each
(j,k,l)\in[p_{1}]\times[p_{2}]\times[p_{3}]
, we have

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}\log({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}+1/2)-{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{*}-\log I\right|\leq 4\nu_{jkl}^{-1}\leq\frac{4e^{B}}{I},\\
&\left\|\log({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}+1/2)-\mathbb{E}\log({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}+1/2)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K_{0}\nu_{jkl}^{-1/2}\leq K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}},\end{split}

(D.23)

where
K_{0}
is the constant defined in Lemma
E.9
. Now we have

\begin{split}&\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})\right\|\leq\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&=\sqrt{\sum_{jkl}\left|\mathbb{E}\log({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}+1/2)-{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{*}-\log I\right|^{2}}\overset{\eqref{eq-TPoisson-bias-subgaussian}}{\leq}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\left(\frac{4e^{B}}{I}\right)^{2}}=\frac{4e^{B}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}}{I},\end{split}

and then

\begin{split}&\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}})\right\|\leq\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\right\|+\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{2})\right\|\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq-Poisson-hpc-2}}{\leq}2K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}}\right)+\frac{4e^{B}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}}{I}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}}\right),\end{split}

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that
I>C\overline{p}e^{B}
, and
\overline{p}=\max\{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}\}
.
Since
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(0)}
is the
r_{k}
leading singular vectors of
\mathcal{M}_{k}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)})
, and
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}=\log\left(({\mathcal{Y}}+1/2)/I\right)\overset{\eqref{eq-Poisson-hpc-1}}{=}\log\left(({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}+1/2)/I\right)={\mathcal{X}}^{*}+{\mathcal{Z}}
, it follows that

\begin{split}&\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{(0)\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-low-rank-matrix-perturbation}}}{\leq}2\sqrt{r_{k}}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}})\right\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}\right).\end{split}

Here
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{(0)}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},p_{k}-r_{k}}
and is orthogonal to
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
. Now we can obtain the upper bound of
\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. Since

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(0)}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&=\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{(0)}}+{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2\perp}^{(0)}}+{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3\perp}^{(0)}}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{k}P_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{(0)}}\right\|_{\rm F}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{(0)\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}\right),\end{split}

we have

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}=\left\|\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(0)}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(0)}}\right\|_{\rm F}+\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(0)}}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&+\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(0)}}\times_{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(0)}}\times_{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(0)}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq-Poisson-hpc-3}}{\leq}C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}\right)+\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}\right)+\frac{4e^{B}\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}}{I}\\
&\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}+\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}\right),\end{split}

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that
I>e^{B}\overline{p}
.

Now we turn to apply Theorem
3.1
. We take the loss function to be the normalized Poisson negative log-likelihood

L({\mathcal{X}})=\frac{1}{I}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{k=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{p_{3}}\left(-{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}+I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{ijk})\right)

with
\nabla L({\mathcal{X}})=-{\mathcal{Y}}/I+\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
. In the convex region
\{{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}:\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq B\}
,
L({\mathcal{X}})
is
e^{B}
-smooth and
e^{-B}
-strongly convex, and by Lemma
E.1
, we know
L({\mathcal{X}})
satisfies RCG
(\alpha,\beta,\mathcal{C})
with
\alpha=\beta=\frac{1}{e^{B}+e^{-B}}
and
\mathcal{C}
is defined according to (
3.3
) and (
4.6
). Then as long as
I\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\kappa^{2}e^{3B}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(p_{-k}r_{k}+p_{k}r_{k}\right)
for some universal constant
C
, the initialization condition
\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq c_{0}\alpha\beta\kappa^{-2}\underline{\lambda}^{2}
in Theorem
3.1
is satisfied. In the meantime, one can calculate that

\xi=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\
\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Y}}/I-\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\right\rangle\overset{\eqref{eq-Poisson-hpc-4}}{\leq}C\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{I/e^{B}}}.

Then
\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\kappa^{4}e^{5B}\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)
implies the signal-noise-ratio condition
\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\alpha^{3}\beta}\xi^{2}
in Theorem
3.1
, and we can obtain the statistical error rate of gradient descent after sufficient steps:

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(T)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\alpha^{2}}\xi^{2}\leq\frac{C\kappa^{4}e^{3B}}{I}\cdot df.

Now it suffices to check (
D.19
)-(
D.22
) actually hold with high probability. First of all, let
A
be the event that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}={\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl},\forall j,k,l
, then we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(A)=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\exists(j,k,l),{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\not\in[\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10},10\nu_{jkl}]\right)\\
&\geq 1-\sum_{j,k,l}\left(\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}<\frac{\nu_{jkl}}{10})+\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}>10\nu_{jkl})\right)\\
&\overset{(a)}{\geq}1-2\sum_{j,k,l}\exp\left(-\frac{4}{5}\nu_{jkl}\right)\geq 1-2\sum_{jkl}\exp\left({-\frac{2}{5}Ie^{-B}}\right)\\
&=1-2\exp\left(\log(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})-\frac{2e^{-B}}{5}I\right)\overset{(b)}{\geq}1-\frac{C}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}.\end{split}

Here (a) comes from applying Poisson’s tail bound (Lemma
E.8
) and (b) is true as long as
I>Ce^{B}\log(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})
, thus (
D.19
) holds with probability at least
1-\frac{C}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}
. By Lemma
E.9
,
\log\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})+1/2\right)-\mathbb{E}\log(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})+1/2)
has independent sub-Gaussian entries with
\psi_{2}
norm bounded by
K_{0}\frac{e^{B}}{I}
, then by
Vershynin 2010
, we have

\begin{split}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\right\|\leq K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}}+t\right)\end{split}

hold with probability at least
1-e^{-t^{2}}
. By setting
t=\sqrt{p_{k}}
, we have (
D.20
) hold with probability at least
1-e^{-\underline{p}}
. Next, for (
D.21
), note that

\begin{split}\sup_{\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\|\mathcal{S}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}}_{1},\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle.\end{split}

Since each entry of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}
has independent sub-Gaussian entry with
\psi_{2}
norm bounded by
C_{0}\frac{e^{B}}{I}
, by Lemma
E.5
, we have:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\sup_{\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}>C_{1}\sqrt{df}\right)\leq 2\exp(-c\cdot df),\end{split}

which gives (
D.21
). Finally, by Lemma
E.10
, we know that (
D.22
) holds with probability at least
1-c/(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})
. Thus, applying union bounds on the above probabilistic events, we know that (
D.19
)-(
D.22
) hold with probability at least
1-c/(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})
and the proof is finished.
\quad\quad\square

D.4
Proof of Theorem
4.5

The proof is very similar to that of Theorem
4.3
. Let
g(\mathcal{P};{\cal N})
be a mapping from
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
to
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
, such that

[g(\mathcal{P};{\cal N})]_{jkl}=\log\left(\frac{\mathcal{P}_{jkl}+1/(2{\cal N}_{jkl})}{1-\mathcal{P}_{jkl}+1/(2{\cal N}_{jkl})}\right),

then we have
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}_{jkl}=g(\hat{\mathcal{P}},{\cal N})
with
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}={\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}/{\cal N}_{jkl}
for any
j,k,l
. Now we define

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{jkl}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}1_{\left\{|\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}-\mathcal{P}_{jkl}|\leq\mathcal{P}_{jkl}/2\right\}}+\mathcal{P}_{jkl}1_{\left\{|\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}-\mathcal{P}_{jkl}|>\mathcal{P}_{jkl}/2\right\}},

where
\mathcal{P}_{jkl}:=s({\mathcal{X}}^{*}_{jkl})
, and further denote
{\mathcal{Z}}={\mathcal{Z}}_{1}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
with

{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}=g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N})-\mathbb{E}g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N}),\quad{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}=\mathbb{E}g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N})-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}.

Again, we first impose the following conditions for the deterministic analysis, and then verify they actually hold with high probability.

(
A_{1}
)

\mathcal{P}_{jkl}^{\prime}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl},\quad\forall(j,k,l)\in[p_{1}]\times[p_{2}]\times[p_{3}]

(D.24)

(
A_{2}
)

\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}}_{1})\right\|\leq 2K_{0}\sqrt{\frac{e^{2B}}{N}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-k}}+\sqrt{p_{k}}\right),\quad k=1,2,3

(D.25)

(
A_{3}
)

\sup_{\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1,k=1,2,3}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\times_{1}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C_{1}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{N/e^{2B}}},

(D.26)

(
A_{4}
)

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\
\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle-\hat{\mathcal{P}}+s({\mathcal{X}}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\right\rangle\leq C_{2}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{N}}.

(D.27)

By Lemma
E.11
, we know that given
N>C_{0}Be^{3B}
for some universal constant
C_{0}
,

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}[g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N})]_{jkl}-{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{*}\right|\leq C\frac{e^{2B}}{N},\\
&\left\|g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N})-\mathbb{E}g(\mathcal{P}^{\prime};{\cal N})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{2B}}{N}}.\end{split}

Then one can combine this with conditions (
D.24
), (
D.25
) and (
D.26
) to obtain the initialization error by the same proof technique we used in Theorem
4.3
:

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{2B}}{N}}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\sqrt{p_{k}r_{k}}+\sqrt{p_{-k}r_{k}}\right),

as long as
N\geq C\overline{p}e^{3B}
.

Now we start to check the conditions in Theorem
3.1
. Notice that the loss function
L({\mathcal{X}})=-\sum_{jkl}\left(\mathcal{P}_{jkl}{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}+\log(1-s({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}))\right)
is of
\frac{1}{e^{B}+3}
-smoothness and
\frac{1}{4}
-strong convexity, then by Lemma
E.1
, we can set

\alpha=\frac{1}{2(e^{B}+3)}<\frac{\frac{1}{4(e^{B}+3)}}{\frac{1}{e^{B}+3}+1/4},\quad\beta=\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{1}{\frac{1}{e^{B}+3}+1/4}

and
L({\mathcal{X}})
satisfies RCG
(\alpha,\beta,\mathcal{C})
with
\mathcal{C}
defined according to (
3.3
) and (
4.6
).
In addition, we can also evaluate the scale of
\xi
:

\xi:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\\
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}),\\
\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle-\hat{\mathcal{P}}+s({\mathcal{X}}^{*}),\mathcal{T}\right\rangle\overset{\eqref{eq-Binomial-hpc-4}}{\leq}C_{2}\frac{\sqrt{df}}{\sqrt{N}}.

Then as we have
N\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\kappa^{2}e^{3B}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(p_{k}r_{k}+p_{-k}r_{k}\right)
, it follows that

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(0)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C\frac{e^{2B}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(p_{k}r_{k}+p_{-k}r_{k}\right)\leq\frac{c\alpha\beta\underline{\lambda}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}.

Thus the initialization condition is meet. In the meantime, one can also check that the signal-noise-ratio condition
\underline{\lambda}^{2}\geq C\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\alpha^{3}\beta}\xi^{2}
is also satisfied when
\kappa
and
B
are treated as constants. Thus by applying Theorem
3.1
, we obtain

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{T}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\xi^{2}=C\kappa^{2}e^{2B}\frac{df}{N}.

Now we are going to show that conditions (
D.24
)-(
D.27
) hold with high probability. First of all,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\right)&=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\exists(j,k,l),\mathcal{P}_{jkl}\neq\mathcal{P}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right)\\
&\geq 1-\sum_{j,k,l}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}\leq\frac{\mathcal{P}_{jkl}}{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{jkl}\geq\frac{3}{4}\mathcal{P}_{jkl}\right)\right)\\
&\geq 1-2\sum_{j,k,l}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}{\cal N}_{jkl}\mathcal{P}_{jkl}^{2}\right)\\
&\geq 1-2p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\exp\left(-cNe^{-2B}\right)\\
&\geq 1-\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}},\end{split}

where the last but one inequality comes from the assumption that
\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\infty}<B
and
\mathcal{P}=s({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
, and the last inequality comes from the condition that
N\geq Ce^{2B}\log(\overline{p})
, so (
D.24
) holds with probability at least
1-\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}
. We can also prove that (
D.25
), (
D.26
), and (
D.27
) hold with probability at least
1-1/p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}
as we did for the proof of (
D.20
), (
D.21
) and (
D.22
) in Theorem
4.3
, and we omitted them here.

\square

D.5
Proof of Theorem
4.4

Since we assume
\max_{k}r_{k}\leq\min_{k}\sqrt{p_{k}}
, it suffices to show the following inequalities:

\inf_{\hat{{\mathcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\hat{{\mathcal{X}}}-{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq cI^{-1}p_{k}r_{k},\quad\forall k=1,2,3

(D.28)

We only need to prove (
D.28
) for
k=1
. First of all, we let
{\mathbf{V}}\in\{-1,1\}^{\lfloor\frac{p_{k}}{2}\rfloor\times r_{k}}
such that each entry are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables (i.e.,
\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{V}}_{ij}=1)=\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{V}}_{ij}=-1)=1/2,~\forall i,j
). Then by
Vershynin 2010
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\lfloor\frac{p_{1}}{2}}\rfloor-\sqrt{r_{1}}-t\leq\sigma_{r_{1}}({\mathbf{V}})\leq\left\|{\mathbf{V}}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\lfloor\frac{p_{1}}{2}}\rfloor+\sqrt{r_{1}}+t\right)>1-e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}.

Taking
t=c_{0}\sqrt{r_{1}}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{0}
, since the above probability is positive, we know that there exists a
{\mathbf{V}}_{0}\in\{-1,1\}^{\lfloor\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rfloor\times r_{1}}
, such that

\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{0}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\lfloor\frac{p_{1}}{2}}\rfloor+(1+c_{0})\sqrt{r_{1}}\leq\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}},

and

\begin{split}\sigma_{r_{1}}({\mathbf{V}}_{0})&\geq\sqrt{\lfloor\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rfloor}-(1+c_{0})\sqrt{r_{1}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}-1}{2}}-(1+c_{0})C_{2}^{-1/2}r_{1}\\
&\geq\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}-1}{2}}-(1+c_{0})C_{1}C_{2}^{-1/2}p_{1}^{1/2}\\
&\geq\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}-1}{2}}-c_{0}p_{1}^{1/2}\geq\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{3}}.\end{split}

Here we obtain the last but one inequality by taking
C_{2}>C_{1}^{2}/c_{0}^{2}
.

In addition, for
i=1,2,\ldots,N
, let
{\mathbf{U}}^{(i)}\in\{-1,1\}^{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil\times r_{1}}
be the i.i.d. copy of
{\mathbf{V}}_{0}
, and we denote

\Omega^{(ij)}=\left\{1\leq a\leq\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil,1\leq b\leq r_{1}:{\mathbf{U}}^{(i)}_{ab}\neq{\mathbf{U}}^{(j)}_{ab}\right\}.

Since
\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|\sim\text{Binomial}\left(\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1},\frac{1}{2}\right)
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4}\leq\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|\leq\frac{3\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4},\forall i\neq j\right)\\
&=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\exists i\neq j,\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|<\frac{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4}~\text{or}~\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|>\frac{3\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4},\forall i\neq j\right)\\
&\geq 1-N(N-1)/2\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|<\frac{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|>\frac{3\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}{4}\right)\right)\\
&=1-\frac{N(N-1)}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\left|\Omega^{(ij)}\right|}{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}}-\frac{1}{2}\right|>\frac{1}{4}\right)\\
&\geq 1-N(N-1)e^{-\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}/32}\\
&\geq 1-N(N-1)e^{-p_{1}r_{1}/64}.\end{split}

(D.29)

In the meantime, by
Vershynin 2010
, we also have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil}+\sqrt{r_{1}}+t,~\forall 1\leq i\leq N\right)\\
&=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\exists i\in[N],\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|>\sqrt{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil}+\sqrt{r_{1}}+t\right)\\
&\geq 1-Ne^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}.\end{split}

Taking
t=\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}}
, we see that

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|\leq 2\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}},~\forall 1\leq i\leq N\right)\geq 1-Ne^{-p_{1}r_{1}/2}.

(D.30)

Combining (
D.29
) and (
D.30
), we know that with probability at least
1-N(N-1)e^{-p_{1}r_{1}/64}-Ne^{-p_{1}r_{1}/2}
,

\begin{split}\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1}\leq\left\|{\mathbf{U}}^{(i)}-{\mathbf{U}}^{(j)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 3\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil r_{1},\quad 1\leq i<j\leq N,\end{split}

(D.31)

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|\leq 2\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}},\quad 1\leq i\leq N.\end{split}

(D.32)

By taking
N=e^{cp_{1}r_{1}}
for some small constant
c
, the probability defined in (
D.30
) is positive and thus there exists
\left\{{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)},\ldots,{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(N)}\right\}\subset\{-1,1\}^{\lceil\frac{p_{1}}{2}\rceil\times r_{1}}
such that (
D.31
) and (
D.32
) hold.

Now we let
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}}
be a fixed core tensor such that

\frac{B}{C\sqrt{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}}\leq\min\limits_{k}\sigma_{r_{k}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\right)\leq\max\limits_{k}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\leq\frac{B}{3\sqrt{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}}

(D.33)

for some
C>3
, and let
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
be the orthogonal matrix such that
\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\leq\frac{\mu_{k}r_{k}}{p_{k}}
for
k=2,3
. Let
{\mathcal{X}}^{(i)}=\delta\cdot\llbracket\mathcal{S};\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
for some
0<\delta<1
with

\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\\
{\mathbf{V}}_{0}\end{bmatrix}.

Let
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{1}^{(i)}=\hat{{\mathbf{U}}}_{1}^{(i)}{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}
be the QR-decomposition of
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}
where
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
is an orthogonal matrix. Thus we can rewrite
{\mathcal{X}}^{(i)}=\llbracket\delta\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)};\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
. Note that by construction,
\sigma_{r_{1}}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right)\geq\sigma_{r_{1}}\left({\mathbf{V}}_{0}\right)\geq\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{3}}
, and we have

\begin{split}\left\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}&=\max_{i}\left\|e_{i}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\max_{i}\left\|e_{i}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\left({\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\leq\max_{i}\frac{\left\|e_{i}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\sigma_{r_{1}}^{2}\left({\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}\right)}\leq\frac{\mu_{k}r_{k}}{p_{k}}.\end{split}

Here the last inequality comes from the facts that
\left\|e_{i}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|_{2}^{2}=r_{k}
for each
i
and
\sigma_{r_{1}}\left({\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}\right)=\sigma_{r_{1}}\left(\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right)
.

In the meantime, we also have

\begin{split}&\max_{k}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\delta\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)})\right\|\leq\max_{k}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\cdot\left\|{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}\right\|\\
&\qquad\overset{\eqref{ineq-lowerbound-core}}{\leq}\frac{B}{3\sqrt{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\frac{B}{3\sqrt{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}}\left(\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i)}\right\|+\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{0}\right\|\right)\\
&\qquad\overset{\eqref{ineq-U-property-2}}{\leq}\frac{B}{\sqrt{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}}\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}}\leq B\sqrt{\frac{\Pi_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}}{\Pi_{k=1}^{3}\mu_{k}r_{k}}},\end{split}

where the last inequality comes from the assumption that
\max_{k}r_{k}\leq\min_{k}\sqrt{p_{k}}
. Thus we have
{\mathcal{X}}^{(i)}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}
for each
i\in[N]
.

Now we provide the KL-divergence between
{\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{1})}\sim{\rm Poisson}(I\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{1})}))
and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{2})}\sim{\rm Poisson}(I\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{2})}))
for
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
. For any two Poisson distribution
P\sim{\rm Poisson}(\lambda_{1})
and
Q\sim{\rm Poisson}(\lambda_{2})
, the KL-divergence between
P
and
Q
is

D_{KL}\left(P||Q\right)=\lambda_{1}\log\left(\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\lambda_{2}}\right)+\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}.

Thus we have

\begin{split}&D_{KL}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{1})}||{\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{2})}\right)=\sum_{j,k,l}D_{KL}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{1})}_{jkl}||{\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{2})}_{jkl}\right)\\
=&I\sum_{j,k,l}\left(\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(i_{1})})\left({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{1})}_{jkl}-{\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{2})}_{jkl}\right)+\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{2})}_{jkl})-\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{1})}_{jkl})\right)\\
\overset{(a)}{=}&I\sum_{j,k,l}\frac{1}{2}\exp\left(\xi^{(i_{1},i_{2})}_{jkl}\right)\left({\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{1})}_{jkl}-{\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{2})}_{jkl}\right)^{2}\\
\overset{(b)}{\leq}&\frac{1}{2}Ie^{B}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(i)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{(j)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}Ie^{B}\delta^{2}\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}Ie^{B}\delta^{2}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\right\|^{2}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\overset{\eqref{ineq-lowerbound-core}}{\leq}&I\frac{\delta^{2}B^{2}e^{B}}{18\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{2})}\right\|_{{\rm F}}^{2}\overset{\eqref{ineq-U-property-1}}{\leq}I\frac{\delta^{2}B^{2}e^{B}}{12\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}p_{1}r_{1}.\end{split}

(D.34)

Here (a) is obtained by applying second order Taylor’s expansion on exponential function, and
\xi_{jkl}^{(i_{1},i_{2})}
is some real number between
{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(i_{1})}
and
{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(i_{2})}
; (b) comes from the fact that
{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(i_{1})}\vee{\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}^{(i_{2})}\leq B
by construction.

Besides,
\forall i_{1}\neq i_{2}
, we also have

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{1})}-{\mathcal{X}}^{(i_{2})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}&=\delta^{2}\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\geq\delta^{2}\sigma_{r_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\right)^{2}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq-lowerbound-core}}{\geq}\frac{\delta^{2}B^{2}}{C^{2}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(i_{1})}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(i_{2})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\overset{\eqref{ineq-U-property-1}}{\geq}\frac{\delta^{2}B^{2}}{4C^{2}\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}p_{1}r_{1}.\end{split}

(D.35)

Then by generalized Fano Lemma
(
Yang and Barron 1999
)
,

\inf_{\hat{\mathcal{X}}}\sup_{{\mathcal{X}}\in\{{\mathcal{X}}^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{N}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\hat{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq c\frac{\delta^{2}B^{2}}{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}p_{1}r_{1}\left(1-\frac{IB^{2}e^{B}\delta^{2}p_{1}r_{1}/(12\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3})+\log 2}{cp_{1}r_{1}-1}\right).

By setting
\delta^{2}=\frac{c\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}{100IB^{2}e^{B}}
, we obtain (
D.29
).
\square

D.6
Proof of Theorem
4.6

The proof of Theorem
4.6
is similar to that of Theorem
4.4
. We construct the same
\left\{{\mathcal{X}}^{(1)},\ldots{\mathcal{X}}^{(N)}\right\}_{i=1}^{N}
as we did in the proof of Theorem
4.4
.
Notice that for any two binomial distributions
P=\text{Binomial}(n,p)
,
Q=\text{Binomial}(n,q)
, the KL-divergence between
P
and
Q
is

D_{KL}\left(P||Q\right)=\log\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)np+\log\left(\frac{1-p}{1-q}\right)n(1-p).

Now let
P_{1}=\text{Binomial}(n,s(x_{1}))
and
P_{2}=\text{Binomial}(n,s(x_{2}))
, for some
-B\leq x_{1}\leq x_{2}\leq B
where
s(x)=1/(1+e^{-x})
, then the KL-divergence of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
is

\begin{split}&D_{KL}(P_{1}||P_{2})=n\left(\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-x_{2}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}\right)\frac{1}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}+\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-x_{2}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}e^{x_{2}-x_{1}}\right)\frac{e^{-x_{1}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}\right)\\
&=n\left(\log\left(\frac{1+e^{-x_{2}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}\right)+\frac{e^{-x_{1}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}(x_{2}-x_{1})\right)\\
&=n\left(\log\left(1+e^{-x_{2}}\right)-\log\left(1+e^{-x_{1}}\right)+\frac{e^{-x_{1}}}{1+e^{-x_{1}}}(x_{2}-x_{1})\right)\\
&\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{ne^{-\xi}}{(1+e^{-\xi})^{2}}=\frac{n}{2+e^{\xi}+e^{-\xi}}\leq\frac{n}{4}.\end{split}

Here
(a)
comes from the second order Taylor’s expansion of
f(x)=\log\left(1+e^{-x}\right)
.
Then we obtain

D_{KL}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{1})}||{\mathcal{Y}}^{(i_{2})}\right)\leq c\delta^{2}\max_{jkl}{\cal N}_{jkl}p_{1}r_{1}\leq c\delta^{2}\min_{jkl}{\cal N}_{jkl}p_{1}r_{1},

given
{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{(i)}\sim\text{Binomial}({\cal N}_{jkl},s({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl}))
independently. By the similar argument in the proof of Theorem
4.4
, we can show

\inf_{\hat{{\mathcal{X}}}}\sup_{{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{F}_{{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}}\left\|\hat{{\mathcal{X}}}-{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq cN^{-1}\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right),

and the proof is finished.
\square

D.7
Proof of Proposition
5.1

Assume
\sigma=1
without loss of generality. We only prove for
k=1
, while the proof for other modes follows in the same way. We prove the two scenarios separately.

(a)

Sub-Gaussian tensor PCA.
Let
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}}
be the left singular subspace of
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},p_{1}-r_{1}}
be the orthogonal subspace of
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
. Let
{\mathcal{Z}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
and define the event:

A=\left\{\frac{9}{10}\sqrt{p_{-1}}\leq\sigma_{p_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\right)\leq\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\frac{11}{10}\sqrt{p_{-1}}\right\}.

Since
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})
has independent mean-zero unit-variance sub-Gaussian random variables, by the concentration of singular values of random matrix
(
Vershynin 2010
, Corollary 5.35)
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{p_{-1}}-\sqrt{p_{1}}-t\leq\sigma_{p_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\right)\leq\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\sqrt{p_{-1}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}+t\right)\geq 1-Ce^{-ct^{2}}.

Since
p_{1}\leq cp_{-1}
, taking
t=c\sqrt{p_{1}}
in the above inequality yields that
\mathbb{P}(A)\geq 1-2e^{-cp_{-1}}
.
Now we provide a lower bound for
\sigma_{p_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)
assuming
A
holds. By definition,

\begin{split}\sigma_{p_{1}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)&=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\
\|u\|=1\end{subarray}}\left\|u^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\
\|u\|=1\end{subarray}}\left(\left\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Since

\begin{split}\left\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}&\geq\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\sigma_{r_{1}}^{2}\left({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)\\
&\geq\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\left(\sigma_{r_{1}}\left({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)-\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\|\right)^{2}\\
&\geq\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot(\underline{\lambda}-1.1\sqrt{p_{-1}})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\underline{\lambda}^{2}\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\left\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{2}^{2}&=\left\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\right\|_{2}^{2}\\
&\geq\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}^{\top}\|\cdot\sigma_{p_{1}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\right)\geq\frac{4p_{-1}}{5}\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}\|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

we further have

\begin{split}\sigma_{p_{1}}^{2}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)\geq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}\\
\|u\|=1\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}{2}\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\|+\frac{4p_{-1}}{5}\|u^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1\perp}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\geq\frac{4p_{-1}}{5}.\end{split}

(D.36)

Recall
\delta_{1}
is the median of non-zero singular values of
\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})
, we then have

\begin{split}\delta_{1}\geq\sigma_{p_{1}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{{\mathcal{X}}}))\geq\frac{9\sqrt{p_{-1}}}{10}.\end{split}

On the other hand, since
r_{1}=o(p_{1})
,

\delta_{1}\leq\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)\leq\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\frac{11}{10}\sqrt{p_{-1}}.

Thus,

\begin{split}\sigma_{r_{1}+1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}))&\leq 1.5\delta_{1},\\
\sigma_{r_{1}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{{\mathcal{X}}}))&\geq\underline{\lambda}-\frac{11\sqrt{p_{-1}}}{10}\\
&\geq 2\sqrt{p_{-1}}\geq 1.5\delta_{1}.\\
\end{split}

(D.37)

(
D.37
) implies
\hat{r}_{1}=r_{1}
and the proof is finished.

(b)

Tensor regression.
Following the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we assume each entry of
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
comes from i.i.d.
N(0,1/n)
and
\varepsilon\sim N(0,\frac{1}{n}{\mathbf{I}}_{n})
. Then we have
\tilde{{\mathcal{X}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}{\mathcal{A}}_{i}={\mathcal{A}}^{*}(y)
. Define
{\mathcal{Z}}:=\tilde{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{A}}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})={\mathcal{A}}^{*}(\varepsilon)
and define the linear operator
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}:\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{-1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}({\mathbf{X}})\right)_{i}=\left\langle\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{A}}_{i}),{\mathbf{X}}\right\rangle.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}
be the adjoint operator of
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
. We consider the following events:

\begin{split}A_{1}&:=\left\{\frac{9}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\leq\sigma_{p_{1}}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}}))\leq\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\frac{11}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right\},\\
A_{2}&:=\left\{(1-\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}})\leq\frac{\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}({\mathbf{X}})\|_{2}^{2}}{\|{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}\leq(1+\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}})~~\text{for any rank-$2r_{1}$ matrix ${\mathbf{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{-1}}$}\right\},\\
A_{3}&:=\left\{\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right)\leq\frac{11}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\right\},\\
A_{4}&:=\left\{\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right)\geq\frac{9}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\right\}.\end{split}

We first show that each of the above events hold with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
.

•

A_{1}
: Note that conditional on
\varepsilon
,
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{Z}})={\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon)
has i.i.d. mean-zero normal entries with variance
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}
. Therefore, by random matrix theory, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\geq\frac{\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-1}}-\sqrt{p_{1}}-t\right)\Big|\varepsilon\right)\geq 1-2e^{-t^{2}/2},\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\right\|\leq\frac{\|\varepsilon\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\sqrt{p_{-1}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}+t\right)\Big|\varepsilon\right)\geq 1-2e^{-t^{2}/2},\\
\end{split}

(D.38)

In addition, by the concentration of Chi-square random variable
(
Birgé 2001
, Lemma 8.1)
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\geq 1+2\sqrt{x/n}+2x/n\right)\leq e^{-x},\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\|\varepsilon\|_{2}^{2}\leq 1-2\sqrt{x/n}\right)\leq e^{-x}.\end{split}

Taking
x=cn
, we have with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cp_{-1})
that
\|\varepsilon\|_{2}\geq 9/10
. Now we specify
t=c\sqrt{p_{-1}}
in (
D.38
) and obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\geq\frac{4}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right)\\
\geq&\mathbb{P}\left(\|\varepsilon\|\geq\frac{4}{5}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\geq\frac{4}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\Big|\|\varepsilon\|\geq 1/2\right)\\
\geq&(1-e^{-cp_{-1}})\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\geq\|\varepsilon\|\cdot\frac{8}{9}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\Big|\|\varepsilon\|\geq 1/2\right)\\
\geq&(1-e^{-cp_{-1}})(1-2e^{-cp_{-1}})\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}.\end{split}

(D.39)

Similarly one can prove
\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon)\right\|\leq\frac{6}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right)\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
. Thus, we have proved
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
.

•

A_{2}
: By
(
Recht, Fazel and
Parrilo 2010
, Lemma 4.3)
, when
n\geq C\kappa p_{-1}r_{1}^{3/2}\log r_{1}
, one has
\mathbb{P}(A_{2})\geq 1-\exp(-cp_{-1}r_{1})
.

•

A_{3},A_{4}
: Since
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{A}}_{i})
has i.i.d. Gaussian entries and
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))=r_{1}
, we can assume that the last
p_{1}-r_{1}
rows of
\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
are zeros without loss of generality. Therefore, we can write

\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\\
\mathbf{O}\end{bmatrix},\qquad\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{A}}_{i})=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\\
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\end{bmatrix}

for
{\mathbf{X}}_{1},{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times p_{-1}}
,
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}-r_{1}\times p_{-1}}
. Then we have

\begin{split}&\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right)=\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\\
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\end{bmatrix}\right)\\
&\qquad=\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},p_{1}-r_{1}}}\sup_{u\in{\mathbf{U}}}\left\|u^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\\
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right\|\end{split}

(D.40)

and

\begin{split}&\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right)=\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}}}\inf_{u\in{\mathbf{U}}}\left\|u^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\\
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{2}\\
&\qquad\geq\sup_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1}-r_{1},r_{1}}}\inf_{u\in{\mathbf{U}}}\left\|u^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right\|_{2}\\
&\qquad=\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right).\end{split}

(D.41)

Recall that
{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}
and
{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}
are independent. Conditional on
{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}
is a
(p_{1}-r_{1})
-by-
p_{-1}
random matrix with i.i.d.
N\left(0,\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\right)
entries where
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i_{1}},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle^{2}
. Therefore, by random matrix theory,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right\|\leq\tilde{\sigma}(\sqrt{p_{-1}}-\sqrt{p_{1}-r_{1}}+t)\Bigg|\{{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)\leq 1-2e^{-t^{2}/2},\\
&\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right)\geq\tilde{\sigma}(\sqrt{p_{-1}}-\sqrt{p_{1}-r_{1}}-t)\Bigg|\{{\mathbf{A}}_{i1}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)\leq 1-2e^{-t^{2}/2}.\end{split}

On the other hand, since
n^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}/\|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sim\chi_{n}^{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\leq\frac{\|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{n}\left(1+2\sqrt{x/n}+2x/n\right)\right)&\geq 1-e^{-x},\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\geq\frac{\|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{n}\left(1-2\sqrt{x/n}\right)\right)&\geq 1-e^{-x}.\end{split}

Combining the concentrations inequalities above and following the same argument as (
D.39
), we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right)\geq\frac{9}{10}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right)&\leq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}},\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\mathbf{A}}_{i1},{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle{\mathbf{A}}_{i2}\right\|\leq\frac{11}{10}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\|_{\rm F}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right)&\leq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}.\\
\end{split}

(D.42)

(
D.40
),(
D.41
) and (
D.42
) together imply that
\mathbb{P}(A_{3})\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
,
\mathbb{P}(A_{4})\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
.

By union bound, we know that
\mathbb{P}\left(A_{1}\cap A_{2}\cap A_{3}\cap A_{4}\right)\geq 1-Ce^{-cp_{-1}}
. Now we show that
\hat{r}_{1}=r_{1}
under the event
A_{1}\cap A_{2}\cap A_{3}\cap A_{4}
.
On the one hand,

\begin{split}\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)&=\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))+{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon))\right)\\
&\leq\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))\right)+\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon)\right\|\\
&\overset{A_{1},A_{3}}{\leq}\frac{11}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}(\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}+1)\leq\frac{6}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

Here in the last inequality we use the assumption that
1=\sigma\leq c\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}
. Meanwhile,

\begin{split}\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)&\geq\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right)-\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon)\right\|\\
&\overset{A_{1},A_{3}}{\geq}\frac{9}{10}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\left(\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}-1\right)\geq\frac{4}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

Consequently, the median
\delta_{1}
satisfies

\begin{split}1.5\delta_{1}\geq 1.5\sigma_{p_{1}-r_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right)\geq\frac{6}{5}\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}\geq\sigma_{r_{1}+1}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{\mathcal{X}})\right).\end{split}

(D.43)

On the other hand,

\begin{split}&\sigma_{r_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))\right)=\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}-1}}\left\|({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))\right\|\\
&\qquad=\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}-1}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\|u\|=1\\
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{-1}},\|v\|=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}){\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})),uv^{\top}\right\rangle\\
&\qquad=\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}-1}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\|u\|=1\\
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{-1}},\|v\|=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})),{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})uv^{\top})\right\rangle\\
&\qquad\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-incoherent-inner-product-difference}}}{\geq}\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}-1}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\|u\|=1\\
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{-1}},\|v\|=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}),({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})uv^{\top}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}\\
&\qquad=\inf_{{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{1},r_{1}-1}}\left\|({\mathbf{I}}_{p_{1}}-{\mathbf{U}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\top})\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|-\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}\\
&\qquad=\sigma_{r_{1}}\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\|-\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}\|\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\|_{\rm F}\geq\lambda_{1}-\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}\kappa\sqrt{\overline{r}}\lambda_{1}\geq\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}.\end{split}

Then,

\begin{split}\sigma_{r_{1}}\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\tilde{{\mathcal{X}}})\right)&\geq\sigma_{r_{1}}\left({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))\right)-\left\|{\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon)\right\|\\
&\geq\frac{\lambda_{1}}{2}-\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\geq\left(\frac{1}{2\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}}}-\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&\geq 2\sqrt{\frac{p_{-1}}{n}}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq 1.5\delta_{1}.\end{split}

(D.44)

Here the last but two inequality comes from the assumption on
n
. Now
\hat{r}_{1}=r_{1}
is implied by (
D.43
) and (
D.44
) and the proof is finished.
\square

Appendix E
Technical Lemmas

We collect the technical lemmas with their proofs in this sections. They are widely used in the proof of the major theorems.

E.1
Proof of Lemma
2.1

Let
{\mathcal{X}}:=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
, we first calculate the partial gradient of
L(\mathcal{S},{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3})
with respect to
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
by chain rule:

\begin{split}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathbf{U}}_{1,ij}}&=\sum_{a=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{b=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{c=1}^{p_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}\cdot\frac{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}{\partial{\mathbf{U}}_{1,ij}}\\
&=\sum_{a=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{b=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{c=1}^{p_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}\cdot\left(I_{\{a=i\}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{l_{3}=1}^{r_{3}}\mathcal{S}_{jl_{2}l_{3}}{\mathbf{U}}_{2,bl_{2}}{\mathbf{U}}_{3,cl_{3}}\right)\\
&=\sum_{b=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{c=1}^{p_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{ibc}}\left(\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{l_{3}=1}^{r_{3}}\mathcal{S}_{jl_{2}l_{3}}{\mathbf{U}}_{2,bl_{2}}{\mathbf{U}}_{3,cl_{3}}\right),\end{split}

where the second identity comes from the following fact:

{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{l_{3}=1}^{r_{3}}\mathcal{S}_{l_{1}l_{2}l_{3}}{\mathbf{U}}_{1,al_{1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{2,bl_{2}}{\mathbf{U}}_{3,cl_{3}}

In the meantime, one can verify that

\left(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\nabla L({\mathcal{X}}))({\mathbf{U}}_{3}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{2})\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})^{\top}\right)_{ij}=\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{p_{3}}\sum_{k_{3}=1}^{r_{2}}\sum_{k_{4}=1}^{r_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{ik_{1}k_{2}}}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}_{2,k_{1}k_{3}}{\mathbf{U}}_{3,k_{2}k_{4}}\mathcal{S}_{jk_{3}k_{4}},

which is exactly what we calculated for
\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathbf{U}}_{1,ij}}
(by changing the order of summation). The partial gradient for
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
can be similarly calculated. For core tensor
\mathcal{S}
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial L}{\partial\mathcal{S}_{ijk}}&=\sum_{a=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{b=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{c=1}^{p_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}\cdot\frac{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}{\partial\mathcal{S}_{ijk}}\\
&=\sum_{a=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{b=1}^{p_{2}}\sum_{c=1}^{p_{3}}\frac{\partial L}{\partial{\mathcal{X}}_{abc}}\cdot{\mathbf{U}}_{1,ai}{\mathbf{U}}_{2,bj}{\mathbf{U}}_{3,ck}\\
&=\left(\llbracket\nabla L({\mathcal{X}});{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{\top}\rrbracket\right)_{ijk},\end{split}

which has finished the proof of this lemma.
\square

E.2
Proof of Proposition
2.1

Let

\left(\hat{\mathcal{S}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\right)=\mathop{\rm arg\min}\limits_{\mathcal{S},{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}L(\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket;D)+\frac{a}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.

We claim
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}
for
k=1,2,3
. Otherwise, consider the QR-decomposition
\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}
where
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
and define
\bar{\mathcal{S}}=b^{-3}\llbracket\hat{\mathcal{S}};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket
,
\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=b\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
. Then clearly
F(\hat{\mathcal{S}},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{3})>F(\bar{\mathcal{S}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{3})
and makes contradiction.

Similarly, For arbitrary rank-
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
tensor
{\mathcal{X}}=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
, we can redecompose it as
{\mathcal{X}}=\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket
such that
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}=b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}}
. Then it follows that

L(\hat{\mathcal{X}};D)=F(\bar{\mathcal{S}},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\leq F(\mathcal{S}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*})=L({\mathcal{X}};D).

Thus,
\hat{\mathcal{X}}=\llbracket\hat{\mathcal{S}};\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{2},\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
is a rank-constraint minimizer of
L({\mathcal{X}};D)
.
~\square

E.3
Lemmas for Main Theory

We collect the technical lemmas that are used to develop Theorem
3.1
in this section.

The first lemma builds the relationship between the standard strongly convex and smooth assumption with RCG condition
3.1
.

Lemma E.1

Consider a continuously differentiable function
f
, given a convex domain
\mathbb{G}
, suppose for any
x,y\in\mathbb{G}
, we have

\frac{m}{2}\left\|x-y\right\|^{2}\leq f(y)-f(x)-\left\langle\nabla f(x),y-x\right\rangle\leq\frac{M}{2}\left\|x-y\right\|^{2},

then

\left\langle\nabla f(x)-\nabla f(y),x-y\right\rangle\geq\frac{mM}{m+M}\left\|x-y\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{m+M}\left\|\nabla f(x)-\nabla f(y)\right\|^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.
See
Nesterov 1998
.
\square

The next lemma establishes the relationship between
\left\|{\mathcal{X}}^{(t)}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}
and
E^{(t)}
in the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Lemma E.2

Suppose
{\mathcal{X}}^{*}=\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket
,
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}=b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}_{r_{k}},k=1,2,3
,
\overline{\lambda}=\max_{k}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|
, and
\underline{\lambda}=\min_{k}\sigma_{r_{k}}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))
. Let
{\mathcal{X}}=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
be another Tucker low-rank tensor with
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times r_{k}}
,
\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\|\leq(1+c_{d})b
, and
\max_{k}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\leq(1+c_{d})\frac{\overline{\lambda}}{b^{3}}
for some constant
c_{d}>0
. Define

\begin{split}E&:=\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}},k=1,2,3}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{S}-\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Then we have

\begin{split}&E\leq b^{-6}\left(7+\frac{12b^{8}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}C_{d}\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2b^{-2}C_{d}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\\
&\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 4b^{6}\left(1+(3+2c_{d})^{2}(1+c_{d})^{4}\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-8}\right)E,\end{split}

where
C_{d}:=1+7\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-8}\left((1+c_{d})^{6}+(1+c_{d})^{4}(2+c_{d})^{2}\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.
First, note that

\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket-\mathcal{S}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}=b^{-3}\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}.

Then we can decompose
\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
into seven terms,

\begin{split}&\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}+{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}-{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}+{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}-{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\\
=&({\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*})+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}\times_{k}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{2}\mathcal{S}\times_{k}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\times_{k+1}({\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1})\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\\
&+\mathcal{S}\times_{1}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\times_{3}({\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}-{\mathbf{U}}_{3}).\end{split}

By the Inequality of arithmetic and geometric means, we further have

\begin{split}&\left\|\mathcal{S}-\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket-\mathcal{S}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\leq&7b^{-6}\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+7b^{-6}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left(\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right)\\
&+7b^{-6}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left(\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\right\|^{2}\right)\\
&+7b^{-6}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathcal{S})\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}-{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\right\|^{2}\\
\leq&7b^{-6}\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+7b^{-6}\left((1+c_{d})^{6}\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-2}+(1+c_{d})^{4}(2+c_{d})^{2}\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-2}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\\
=&7b^{-6}\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}+7\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-8}\left((1+c_{d})^{6}+(1+c_{d})^{4}(2+c_{d})^{2}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

Since the above inequality holds for any orthogonal matrices
{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}
, it follows that

\begin{split}&E=\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k}\times r_{k}},k=1,2,3}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{S}-\mathcal{S}^{*}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top}\times_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\\
&\leq 7b^{-6}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+C_{d}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\end{split}

(E.1)

where
C_{d}=1+7\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-8}\left((1+c_{d})^{6}+(1+c_{d})^{4}(2+c_{d})^{2}\right)
.

Now let
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}
be the SVD of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
, where
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}
,
\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}
, and
\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}={\rm diag}(\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{r_{k}})
is a diagonal matrix. Then we have

\begin{split}&\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}+b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
\leq&2\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}-b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\\
=&2\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-b{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.2)

On the one hand,

\begin{split}&\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-b{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=b^{2}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}/b-{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
=&b^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}}{b}-1\right)^{2}\leq b^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{b^{2}}-1\right)^{2}=b^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\left(\sigma_{i}^{2}-b^{2}\right)^{2}\\
=&b^{-2}\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{2}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=b^{-2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}\tilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{2}\tilde{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\top}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
=&b^{-2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=b^{-2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

(E.3)

Here we use the inequality
(x-1)^{2}\leq(x^{2}-1)^{2}
for any non-negative number
x
.
On the other hand, since
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b
have orthonormal columns and span the left singular subspaces of
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
respectively, we have

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}&=\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}})-\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{\top}\left(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}})-\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right)\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&=\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{k\perp}^{\top}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b)({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b)^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\geq\sigma_{r_{k}}^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{X}}^{*}))\cdot\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\underline{\lambda}^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\end{split}

where
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k}-r_{k},r_{k}}
is the perpendicular orthogonal matrix of
\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}
. Then it follows by
(
Cai and Zhang 2018
, Lemma 1)
that

\begin{split}\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|b\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 2b^{2}\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k\perp}^{\top}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}/b)\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 2b^{2}\frac{\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}.\end{split}

(E.4)

Combing (
E.2
),(
E.3
) and (
E.4
), we have:

\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 2b^{-2}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+4b^{2}\frac{\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}.

Then by (
E.1
), we finally obtain

\begin{split}E&\leq 7b^{-6}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+C_{d}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\min_{{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}}}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\\
&\leq b^{-6}\left(7+\frac{12b^{8}}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}C_{d}\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+2b^{-2}C_{d}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-b^{2}{\mathbf{I}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}.\end{split}

Next, we prove the second inequality. To this end, we now denote

({\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3})=\mathop{\rm arg\min}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{p_{k},r_{k}}\\
k=1,2,3\end{subarray}}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\left\|\mathcal{S}-\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right\}.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{S}^{*}-\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket
and
{\mathbf{H}}_{k}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}
. Then, we have

{\mathcal{X}}^{*}=({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}+\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket)\times_{1}({\mathbf{H}}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top})\times_{2}({\mathbf{H}}_{2}+{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top})\times_{3}({\mathbf{H}}_{3}+{\mathbf{U}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top})

and it follows that

\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}\times_{3}{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left\|\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\right\|_{\rm F}+\left\|\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{U}}^{*}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{H}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
\leq&b^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}\right\|\\
&+\left\|\mathcal{M}_{3}(\mathcal{S})\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{1}\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
\leq&b^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\rm F}+(1+c_{d})^{3}\overline{\lambda}b^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}+(1+c_{d})^{2}(2+c_{d})\overline{\lambda}b^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
=&b^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\rm F}+(3+2c_{d})(1+c_{d})^{2}\overline{\lambda}b^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}.\end{split}

Thus,

\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{X}}^{*}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq 4\left(b^{6}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+(3+2c_{d})^{2}(1+c_{d})^{4}\overline{\lambda}^{2}b^{-2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right).\quad\quad\square

Suppose
s,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}
are real values and
\varepsilon_{s},\varepsilon_{u_{1}},\varepsilon_{u_{2}},\varepsilon_{u_{3}}
are small perturbations, then by simple calculation we can see that

(s+\varepsilon_{s})(u_{1}+\varepsilon_{u_{2}})(u_{1}+\varepsilon_{u_{2}})(u_{3}+\varepsilon_{u_{3}})=su_{1}u_{2}u_{3}+\varepsilon_{s}u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}s\varepsilon_{u_{k}}u_{k+1}u_{k+2}+h_{\varepsilon},

(E.5)

where
h_{\varepsilon}
is a high-order perturbation term. The following lemma can be seen as a tensor version of (
E.5
), which plays a key role in the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

Lemma E.3

Suppose
