{\mathcal{X}}^{*}=\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket
,
{\mathcal{X}}=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\rrbracket
with
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{S}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}}
,
{\mathbf{U}}_{k},{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times r_{k}},{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\in\mathbb{O}_{r_{k}},k=1,2,3
. Let

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{X}}_{k}=\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2},\quad{\mathbf{H}}_{k}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top},\quad k=1,2,3\\
&{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top}\rrbracket,\quad{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{S}^{*}-\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket.\end{split}

Then we have the following decomposition of
{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
:

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{X}}^{*}={\mathcal{X}}_{S}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}({\mathcal{X}}_{k}-{\mathcal{X}})+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon},\quad\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq B_{2}B_{3}^{3/2}+3B_{1}B_{2}B_{3}+3B_{1}^{2}B_{3}.\end{split}

Here,

\begin{split}&B_{1}:=\max_{k}\left\{\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\right\|,\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}\right\|\right\},\quad B_{2}:=\max_{k}\left\{\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})\right\|,\left\|\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S}^{*})\right\|\right\},\\
&B_{3}:=\max\left\{\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right\}.\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
E.3
.

Since
\mathcal{S}^{*}=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket+{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{X}}^{*}=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket+\llbracket{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket.\\
\end{split}

(E.6)

For the first term on the right hand side of (
E.6
), since
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{*}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}+{\mathbf{H}}_{k}
, we have

\begin{split}&\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}+{\mathbf{H}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}+{\mathbf{H}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}+{\mathbf{H}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket\\
=&{\mathcal{X}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}\\
=&{\mathcal{X}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}({\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}^{*}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}-{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2})+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}\\
=&\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{X}}_{k}-2{\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}&=\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{H}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}\rrbracket+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}\\
&=\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*}-{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}};{\mathbf{H}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\rrbracket+\sum_{k=1}^{3}(\mathcal{S}^{*}-{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}})\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}.\end{split}

For the second term on the right hand side of (
E.6
), we have

\begin{split}&\llbracket{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}};{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket\\
=&(\mathcal{S}^{*}-\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3})\times_{1}({\mathbf{H}}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}_{1}^{\top})\times_{2}({\mathbf{H}}_{2}+{\mathbf{U}}_{2}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}^{\top})\times_{3}({\mathbf{H}}_{3}+{\mathbf{U}}_{3}{\mathbf{R}}_{3}^{\top})\\
=&{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}=&\llbracket{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}};{\mathbf{H}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\rrbracket+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top}.\end{split}

Then it follows that

\begin{split}{\mathcal{X}}^{*}={\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{X}}_{k}-3{\mathcal{X}}+({\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}).\end{split}

Since

\begin{split}{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}={\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}=&\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\rrbracket+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}^{*}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{3}{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top},\end{split}

we have

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\varepsilon}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq&\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{H}}_{1},{\mathbf{H}}_{2},{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|\mathcal{S}^{*}\times_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}}\times_{k}{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\times_{k+1}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top}\times_{k+2}{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
=&\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S}^{*})({\mathbf{H}}_{3}\otimes{\mathbf{H}}_{2})^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S}^{*})({\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\otimes{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1})^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}})({\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top})^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
\leq&\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S}^{*})\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{3}\otimes{\mathbf{H}}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}{\mathbf{R}}_{k}^{\top}\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S}^{*})\right\|\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k+2}\otimes{\mathbf{H}}_{k+1}\right\|_{\rm F}\\
&+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\left\|{\mathbf{H}}_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k}({\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{S}})\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+2}^{\top}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}{\mathbf{R}}_{k+1}^{\top}\right\|\\
\leq&B_{3}^{3/2}B_{2}+3B_{3}B_{1}B_{2}+3B_{3}B_{1}^{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have finished the proof.
\quad\square

E.4
Other Technical Lemmas

We collect additional technical lemmas in this section.

Lemma E.4

Suppose
{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
and
{\mathbf{Y}}={\mathbf{X}}+{\mathbf{Z}}
,
{\rm rank}({\mathcal{X}})=r
. If the leading
r
left and right singular vectors of
{\mathbf{Y}}
are
\hat{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathbb{O}_{m,r}
and
\hat{\mathbf{V}}\in\mathbb{O}_{n,r}
, then

\begin{split}&\max\left\{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}\right\|,\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}\right\|\right\}\leq 2\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|,\quad\max\left\{\left\|\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}\right\|_{\rm F},\left\|{\mathbf{X}}\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\perp}\right\|_{\rm F}\right\}\leq\min\{2\sqrt{r}\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|,2\|{\mathbf{Z}}\|_{\rm F}\}.\end{split}

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.
See
(
Zhang and
Han 2018
, Lemma 6)
.
\square

Lemma E.5

Assume all the entries of
{\mathcal{Z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
are independent mean-zero random variables with bounded Orlicz-
\psi_{2}
norm:

\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{ijk}\|_{\psi_{2}}=\sup_{q\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\left(|{\mathcal{Z}}_{ijk}|^{q}\right)^{1/q}/q^{1/2}\leq\sigma.

(E.7)

Then there exist some universal constants
C,c
, such that

\begin{split}&\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}},\|\mathcal{T}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1,\\
{\rm rank}(\mathcal{T})\leq(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{T}\right\rangle\leq C\sigma\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)^{1/2}\end{split}

with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-c\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
E.5
.
First of all,
By
(
Zhang and Xia 2018
, Lemma 7)
, for any
k=1,2,3
, we can construct
\varepsilon
-net
\left\{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(N_{k})}\right\}
for
\left\{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{k}\times r_{k}}:\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1\right\}
such that

\sup_{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}:\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1}\min_{i\leq N_{k}}\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}-{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\varepsilon

with
N_{k}\leq\left(\frac{4+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p_{k}r_{k}}
. Also, we can construct
\varepsilon
-net
\{\mathcal{S}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{S}^{(N_{\mathcal{S}})}\}
for
\{\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}}:\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\}
such that

\sup_{\mathcal{S}:\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1}\min_{i\leq N_{\mathcal{S}}}\left\|\mathcal{S}-\mathcal{S}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\varepsilon

with
N_{\mathcal{S}}\leq((2+\varepsilon)/\varepsilon)^{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}
. We use
{\mathcal{I}}
to denote the index set
[N_{\mathcal{S}}]\times[N_{1}]\times[N_{2}]\times[N_{3}]
. Now suppose we have

\begin{split}\left(\mathcal{S}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{*}\right)=\mathop{\rm arg\max}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\\
{\mathbf{V}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{V}}_{l}\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle,\\
\end{split}

(E.8)

and denote

T:=\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{*}\rrbracket\right\rangle.

Then we can find some index
i=(i_{0},i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})\in{\mathcal{I}}
, such that

\begin{split}\left\|\mathcal{S}^{*}-\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})}\right\|_{\rm F}&\leq\varepsilon,\quad\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{*}-{\mathbf{V}}_{k}^{(i_{k})}\right\|\leq\varepsilon,~k=1,2,3\end{split}

and it follows that for any
j,k,l
, by taking
\varepsilon=1/14
, we have

\begin{split}&T=\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle+T-\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle+\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{*};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{*},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{*}\right\rrbracket-\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle\\
&\leq\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle+\left(3\varepsilon+3\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon^{3}\right)T\\
&\leq\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\left\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\right\rrbracket\right\rangle+T/2\end{split}

and
T\leq 2\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\rrbracket\right\rangle
.

Notice that for any fixed
\mathcal{S}
,
\left\{{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\}_{k=1}^{3}
with
\|\mathcal{S}\|_{\rm F}\leq 1
and
\left\|{\mathbf{V}}_{k}\right\|\leq 1
, we have
\left\|\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{2},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1
. Since
{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}
are independent sub-Gaussian random variable with
\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
, by Hoeffding-type inequality for sub-Gaussian random variables’ summation
(
Vershynin 2010
)
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(i_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(i_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(i_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(i_{3})}\rrbracket\right\rangle\right|\geq t\right)\leq\exp\left(1-\frac{ct^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Then it follows by union bound that

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(T\geq t\right)&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{j_{0},j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\llbracket\mathcal{S}^{(j_{0})};{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{(j_{1})},{\mathbf{V}}_{2}^{(j_{2})},{\mathbf{V}}_{3}^{(j_{3})}\rrbracket\right\rangle\right|\geq\frac{t}{2}\right)\\
&\leq N_{\mathcal{S}}N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}\exp\left(1-\frac{ct^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\right)\\
&\leq\exp\left(1-\frac{ct^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}+C(\varepsilon)\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)\right).\end{split}

Now by taking
t=C\sigma\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}}
, we have with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-c(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k})\right)
,

T\leq C\sigma\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)^{1/2},

which has finished the proof of this lemma.
\quad\square

Lemma E.6

Suppose
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
are Gaussian ensembles with variance
1/n
, i.e., each entry of
{\mathcal{A}}_{i}
comes from
N(0,1/n)
independent. Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be the affine map such that
[{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})]_{i}=\langle{\mathcal{A}}_{i},{\mathcal{X}}\rangle
, and let
{\mathcal{A}}^{*}
be the adjoint operator of
{\mathcal{A}}
. Assume
n>C\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)
, then we have for any rank-
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
tensor
{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
,

\begin{split}\frac{9}{10}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\right\|\leq\frac{11}{9}\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F},\end{split}

(E.9)

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{A}}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{6}{5}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{n}}\vee 1\right)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}\end{split}

(E.10)

hold with probability at least
1-C\exp\left(-c\left(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}\right)\right)
.

Proof of Lemma
E.6
.

By
(
Rauhut, Schneider and
Stojanac 2017
, Theorem 2)
, for any
\delta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

(1-\delta)\|{\mathcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq(1+\delta)\|{\mathcal{X}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}

for arbitrary rank-
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
tensors
{\mathcal{X}}
with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
provided that

n\geq C\delta^{-2}\max\left\{(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}),\log(\varepsilon^{-1})\right\}.

Setting
\delta=1/10
and
\varepsilon=\exp\left(-C(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k})\right)
, (
E.9
) is established.
To prove (
E.10
), we first note that
\|{\mathcal{A}}^{*}\|
is equivalent to the spectral norm of a (scaled) Gaussian random matrix of size
n\times(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})
. By random matrix theory
(
Vershynin 2010
, Corollary 5.35)
, we have with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cp)
,

\left\|\sqrt{n}{\mathcal{A}}^{*}\right\|\leq\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}+\sqrt{n}+\frac{1}{11}(\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\vee\sqrt{n})\leq\frac{12}{11}(\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\vee\sqrt{n}),

then (
E.10
) follows from (
E.9
) and the fact
\left\|{\mathcal{A}}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\right\|\leq\|{\mathcal{A}}^{*}\|\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\right\|
.
\square

Lemma E.7

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a linear map from
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that for any rank-
({\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}+{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2})
tensor
{\mathcal{X}}
,

(1-\delta)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq(1+\delta)\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2},

Then, for all rank-
{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}
tensor
{\mathcal{X}}
and rank-
{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}
tensor
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we have

\left|\langle{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}),{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{Y}})\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}}\rangle\right|\leq\delta\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}.

Proof of Lemma
E.7
.
Without loss of generality we assume
\left\|{\mathcal{X}}\right\|_{\rm F}=\left\|{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}=1
, then the proof simply follows from the application of the parallelogram identity:

\begin{split}&\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}),{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{Y}})\right\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}}\rangle\right|\\
&=\frac{1}{4}\left|\left(\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{Y}})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{Y}})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)-\left(\left\|{\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{4}\left|\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{Y}})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\left\|{\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right|+\left|\left\|{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{Y}})\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right|\\
&\leq\frac{\delta}{4}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{X}}+{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\left\|{\mathcal{X}}-{\mathcal{Y}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\leq\delta.\quad\quad\square\end{split}

Lemma E.8
(Poisson Tail Bound)

Suppose
W\sim{\rm Poisson}(v)
, then for any
x\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(W\geq v+x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{2v}\psi_{Benn}(x/v)\right).

For any
0\leq x\leq v
,

\mathbb{P}\left(W\leq v-x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{2v}\psi_{Benn}(-x/v)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{2v}\right),

where
\psi_{Benn}(t)=\frac{(1+t)\log(1+t)-t}{t^{2}/2}
for
t>-1,t\neq 0
, and
\psi_{Benn}(0)=1
.

Specifically, for any
0<x<2v
, we have

\mathbb{P}(W\geq v+x)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}}{4v}\right).

Proof of Lemma
E.8
.
See
Boucheron, Lugosi and
Massart 2013
.
\square

Lemma E.9
(Bias and Subgaussian of Truncated Poisson Distribution)

Suppose
W\sim\text{Poisson}(\nu)
and
W^{\prime}=W1_{\left\{\frac{1}{10}\nu\leq W\leq 10\nu\right\}}+\nu 1_{\left\{W\not\in[\frac{1}{10}\nu,10\nu]\right\}}
, then for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
that only depends on
\epsilon
, such that for all
\nu\geq C_{\epsilon}
, we have

\left|\mathbb{E}\log(W^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2})-\log\nu\right|\leq 4\nu^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon},

and we can also find some absolute constant
C,K_{0}>0
such that if
\nu\geq C
, then

\left|\mathbb{E}(\log^{2}(W^{\prime}+1/2))-\log^{2}\nu\right|\leq 4/\nu,

\left\|\sqrt{\nu}\left(\log(W^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2})-\mathbb{E}\log(W^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2})\right)\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq K_{0}.

Proof of Lemma
E.9
.
see
(
Shi, Zhou and Zhang 2018
, Lemma 1, Lemma 2, Lemma 3)
.

Lemma E.10

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{ijk}\sim\text{Poisson}(I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{ijk}))
independently, and each entry of
{\mathcal{X}}
is bounded with
|{\mathcal{X}}_{ijk}|\leq B
. Suppose
I\geq C\left(Be^{B}\log\overline{p}\vee 1\right)
, then with probability at least
1-\frac{c}{\overline{p}}
, we have

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\|\leq 1,l=1,2,3\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},{\mathbf{S}}\times_{1}{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}.

where
{\mathcal{Z}}:={\mathcal{Y}}/I-\exp({\mathcal{X}}^{*})
, and
df:=r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}p_{k}r_{k}
.

Proof of Lemma
E.10
.

For each Poisson random variable
{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}
, define

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}={\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}1_{\{{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\leq 3I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\}},

and let
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}/I-\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}/I
. Notice that when
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
, we have
{\mathcal{Z}}={\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}+\mathbb{E}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}-{\mathcal{Y}})/I
. Now for any
t>0
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\|\leq 1\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\right\rangle\right|>t\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\|\leq 1\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}+\mathbb{E}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}-{\mathcal{Y}})/I,\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\right\rangle\right|>t\right)\\
&+\mathbb{P}\left(\exists~(j,k,l),{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\neq{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\right).\end{split}

(E.11)

We first provide an upper bound for the second probability:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\exists~(j,k,l),{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}&\neq{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl})=\mathbb{P}\left(\cup_{j,k,l}\left\{{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\neq{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\right\}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{j,k,l}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\neq{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}\right)=\sum_{j,k,l}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}>3I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\right)\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-Poisson-tail-bound}}}{\leq}\sum_{j,k,l}\exp\left(-I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\right)\\
&\leq p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}\exp\left(-Ie^{-B}\right)\leq c/\overline{p}\end{split}

(E.12)

Here in the last step we use the assumption that
I>Ce^{B}\log\overline{p}
.

Now we study the first probability in (
E.11
), first of all, we have:

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}-{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl})/I\right|=\mathbb{E}({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}1_{\{{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}>3I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\}})/I\\
&\leq\frac{1}{I}\left(\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}>3I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\right)\right)^{1/2}\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-Poisson-tail-bound}}}{\leq}\frac{1}{I}\left(\sqrt{2}I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\right)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})\right)\\
&\leq\sqrt{2}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}Ie^{-B}+B\right)\leq\sqrt{2}\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}B-\frac{1}{2}\log I\right)=\sqrt{\frac{2e^{B}}{I}}.\end{split}

(E.13)

Next, we claim that
\{{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\}
are independent sub-Gaussian random variables. The following inequality bulids the tail bound of
\left|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right|
:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}|>t\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}>t\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}<-t\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}-\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}>It\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}-\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}^{\prime}<-It\right)\\
&\overset{\eqref{eq-lm-xi-poisson-3}}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}>\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-\sqrt{2Ie^{B}}+It\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}<\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-It\right)\end{split}

The upper tail probability can be bounded when
t
takes different values:

•

2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}<t<\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}+2\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})
:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}>\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-\sqrt{2Ie^{B}}+It\right)\\
&\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-Poisson-tail-bound}}}{\leq}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\frac{I^{2}(t-\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}})^{2}}{I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})}\right)\\
&\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\frac{I(t/2)^{2}}{\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{It^{2}}{16e^{B}}\right).\end{split}

•

t>\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}+2\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})
:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}>\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-\sqrt{2Ie^{B}}+It\right)=0.\end{split}

In conclusion, we have

\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}>\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-\sqrt{2Ie^{B}}+It\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{It^{2}}{16e^{B}}\right),\quad\forall t\geq 2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}.

In the meantime, for any
t>0
we have

\mathbb{P}\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{jkl}<\mathbb{E}{\mathcal{Y}}_{jkl}-It\right)\overset{\text{Lemma \ref {lm-Poisson-tail-bound}}}{\leq}\exp\left(-\frac{I^{2}t^{2}}{2I\exp({\mathcal{X}}_{jkl})}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{It^{2}}{2e^{B}}\right).

Thus

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}|>t)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{It^{2}}{16e^{B}}\right),\quad\forall t\geq 2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}.\end{split}

Now for any
q\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right|^{q}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right|>x\right)qx^{q-1}dx\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right|>x\right)qx^{q-1}dx+\int_{2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{Ix^{2}}{16e^{B}}\right)qx^{q-1}dx\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}}qx^{q-1}dx+\int_{0}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{Ix^{2}}{16e^{B}}\right)qx^{q-1}dx\\
&=\left(2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}\right)^{q}+4q\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-y}\left(\frac{4e^{B/2}\sqrt{y}}{\sqrt{I}}\right)^{q-1}\frac{e^{B/2}}{\sqrt{Iy}}dy\\
&=\left(2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}\right)^{q}+4^{q}qe^{qB/2}I^{-q/2}\Gamma(q/2)\\
&\leq\left(2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}\right)^{q}+4^{q}qe^{qB/2}I^{-q/2}\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)^{q/2}\\
&\leq\left(2\sqrt{2I^{-1}e^{B}}+2\sqrt{2}q^{1/q}\sqrt{\frac{qe^{B}}{I}}\right)^{q}.\end{split}

Here the last inequality comes from the fact that
x^{q}+y^{q}\leq(x+y)^{q}
for
q\geq 1
.
Then we can bound the
\psi_{2}
norm of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}
:

\begin{split}\left\|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}\right\|_{\psi_{2}}&=\sup_{q\geq 1}p^{-1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}|{\mathcal{Z}}_{jkl}^{\prime}|^{q}\right)^{1/p}\leq 2\sqrt{2}\sup_{q\geq 1}q^{-1/2}\left(\sqrt{I^{-1}e^{B}}+q^{1/q}\sqrt{\frac{qe^{B}}{I}}\right)\\
&\leq 2\sqrt{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}+e^{\frac{1}{e}}\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}\right)\leq C\sqrt{\frac{e^{B}}{I}}.\end{split}

Now by Lemma
E.5
, we have some universal constant
C
, such that
with probability
1-e^{c\cdot df}
,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\|\leq 1\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}\times_{1}{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\times_{2}{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\times_{3}{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\right\rangle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}.

(E.14)

Combining (
E.13
) and (
E.14
), we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{l}\times r_{l}},\|{\mathbf{W}}_{l}\|\leq 1\\
\mathcal{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\left\|\mathcal{S}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime}+\mathbb{E}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}-{\mathcal{Y}})/I,\llbracket\mathcal{S};{\mathbf{W}}_{1},{\mathbf{W}}_{2},{\mathbf{W}}_{3}\rrbracket\right\rangle\right|>(C+1)\sqrt{\frac{df\cdot e^{B}}{I}}\right)\leq e^{c\cdot df},

(E.15)

and the conclusion follows by combining (
E.11
), (
E.12
) and (
E.15
).
\square

Lemma E.11

Suppose
Y\sim\text{Binomial}(N,s(x))
, where
s(x)=1/(1+e^{-x})
. Let
\hat{p}^{\prime}=\hat{p}1_{\left\{|\hat{p}-p|\leq\frac{p}{2}\right\}}+p1_{\left\{|\hat{p}-p|>\frac{p}{2}\right\}}
and
X=\log\left(\frac{\hat{p}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2N}}{1-\hat{p}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2N}}\right)
where
p=s(x)
,
\hat{p}=Y/N
. Then if
N\geq C|x|e^{3|x|}
, for any
\epsilon>0
, we have universal constants
K_{0}
,
C
, such that

\begin{split}\left|\mathbb{E}X-x\right|\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon},\\
\left\|e^{-|x|}\sqrt{N}\left(X-\mathbb{E}X\right)\right\|_{\phi_{2}}\leq K_{0}.\end{split}

(E.16)

Proof of Lemma
E.11
: To simplify the proof, we only consider the case where
x\leq 0
. Let
g(t)=\log\left(\frac{t+\frac{1}{2N}}{1-t+\frac{1}{2N}}\right),t\in[0,1]
, then we have
X=g(\hat{p}^{\prime})
. We first provide the derivatives of
g(t)
up to order
3
:

\begin{split}&g^{\prime}(t)=\left(t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-1}+\left(1-t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-1},\\
&g^{\prime\prime}(t)=-\left(t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-2}+\left(1-t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-2},\\
&g^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)=2\left(t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-3}+2\left(1-t+\frac{1}{2N}\right)^{-3}.\end{split}

(E.17)

Let
p:=s(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}
, by Taylor’s expansion of
g(t)
at
t_{0}=\frac{N+1}{N}p-\frac{1}{2N}
, we have

g(t)=g(t_{0})+g^{\prime}(t_{0})(t-t_{0})+\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})(t-t_{0})^{2}+\frac{1}{6}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(\xi)(\xi-t_{0})^{3},

(E.18)

where
\xi
is some number between
t_{0}
and
t
. Now we let
f(t)=g(t)-g(t_{0})-g^{\prime}(t_{0})(t-t_{0})-\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(t_{0})(t-t_{0})^{2}
, then one can see that as long as
-\frac{1}{2N}\leq t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\leq t\leq t_{0}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\leq 1+\frac{1}{2N}
(the first and third inequality holds since
N>C|x|e^{3|x|}>Cp^{-3}
), we have some universal constant
C
, such that

\begin{split}&|f(t)|\leq\frac{1}{3}\sup_{t:|t-t_{0}|\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\left(\left|t+\frac{1}{2N}\right|^{-3}+\left|1-t+\frac{1}{2N}\right|^{-3}\right)\left|t-t_{0}\right|^{3}\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{2}{3}\left|\frac{N+1}{N}p-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right|^{-3}N^{-3/2+3\epsilon}\leq Cp^{-3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}\end{split}

(E.19)

with
\epsilon_{1}=3\epsilon
. Here the last inequality comes from the following fact:

\begin{split}\frac{N+1}{N}p-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}&\geq\frac{N+1}{N}p-p^{\frac{3}{2}-3\epsilon}\geq cp.\end{split}

Thus it follows that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left|f(\hat{p})1_{\{t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}<\hat{p}<t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\}}\right|\leq Cp^{-3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}=C(e^{|x|}+1)^{3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}.\end{split}

(E.20)

Then we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left|f(\hat{p})\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left|f(\hat{p})1_{\{t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}<\hat{p}<t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\}}\right|+\mathbb{E}\left|f(\hat{p})1_{\{\hat{p}\in[0,t_{0}-N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}]\cup[t_{0}+N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon},1]\}}\right|\\
&\leq C(e^{|x|}+1)^{3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}+\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|g^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)|\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(|\hat{p}-t_{0}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)\\
&\leq C(e^{|x|}+1)^{3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{32}{3}N^{3}\mathbb{P}\left(|\hat{p}-p|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}-N^{-1}\right)\\
&\leq C(e^{|x|}+1)^{3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}+\frac{32}{3}N^{3}\mathbb{P}\left(|\hat{p}-p|>\frac{1}{2}N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\right)\\
&\leq C(e^{|x|}+1)^{3}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}+CN^{3}e^{-\frac{1}{2}N^{2\varepsilon}}\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}},\end{split}

(E.21)

where the last but one inequality comes from applying the following concentration inequality for Binomial random variable:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|Y-p\right|\geq t\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-2Nt^{2}\right),\quad\forall t\geq 0.

(E.22)

In the mean time, one can calculate that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})-\mathbb{E}f(\hat{p})=g(t_{0})+\frac{2p-1}{(N+1)^{2}p(1-p)}\left(\frac{1}{8p(1-p)}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\\
&=\log(\frac{p}{1-p})+\frac{2p-1}{(N+1)^{2}p(1-p)}\left(\frac{1}{8p(1-p)}-\frac{1}{2}\right).\end{split}

(E.23)

Combining (
E.21
) and (
E.23
), we have

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})-x\right|=\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})-\log\left(\frac{p}{1-p}\right)-\mathbb{E}f(\hat{p})+\mathbb{E}f(\hat{p})\right|\\
&\leq\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})-\log\left(\frac{p}{1-p}\right)-\mathbb{E}f(\hat{p})\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}f(\hat{p})\right|\\
&\leq\frac{2p-1}{(N+1)^{2}p(1-p)}\left(\frac{1}{8p(1-p)}-\frac{1}{2}\right)+Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}\\
&\leq Ce^{2|x|}N^{-2}+Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}\\
&\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}.\end{split}

Now we calculate the bias of
X=g(\hat{p}^{\prime})
. First,

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})\right|=\left|g(p)\mathbb{P}\left(|\hat{p}-p|\geq\frac{p}{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})1_{\{|\hat{p}-p|\leq\frac{p}{2}\}}-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})\right|\\
\leq&\left|g(p)\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\leq\frac{p}{2})-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})1_{\{\hat{p}\leq\frac{p}{2}\}}\right|+\left|g(p)\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\geq\frac{3p}{2})-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})1_{\{\hat{p}\geq\frac{3p}{2}\}}\right|\\
\leq&\max\left\{g(p)\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\leq\frac{p}{2}),\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})1_{\{\hat{p}\leq\frac{p}{2}\}}\right\}+\max\left\{g(p)\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\geq\frac{3p}{2}),\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})1_{\{\hat{p}\geq\frac{3p}{2}\}}\right\}\\
\overset{(a)}{\leq}&\max\left\{g(p)e^{-\frac{1}{2}Np^{2}},\left(\mathbb{E}g^{2}(\hat{p})\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\leq\frac{p}{2})\right)^{1/2}\right\}\\
&+\max\left\{g(p)e^{-\frac{1}{2}Np^{2}},\left(\mathbb{E}g^{2}(\hat{p})\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{P}(\hat{p}\geq\frac{3p}{2})\right)^{1/2}\right\}\\
\leq&2\max\left\{g(p)e^{-\frac{1}{2}Np^{2}},\left(\mathbb{E}g^{2}(\hat{p})\right)^{1/2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}Np^{2}}\right\}\\
&\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\log(2N+1)e^{-\frac{1}{2}Np^{2}}\leq 2\log(2N+1)e^{-\frac{1}{2}N^{1/3}}\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}.\end{split}

Here, we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality for (a), and apply the uniform bound
g(p)\leq\log(2N+1)
for (b). Combining two inequalities above, we obtain

\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-x\right|\leq\left|\mathbb{E}g(p^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p})-x\right|\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}},

which gives the first inequality of (
E.16
).

Now we prove the second inequality of (
E.16
). We denote
D=g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})
for convenience. By what we have proved, we have
\left|\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-x\right|\leq Ce^{3|x|}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}\leq Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}
, thus we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(|e^{x}\sqrt{N}D|>t\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(e^{x}\sqrt{N}D>t\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(e^{x}\sqrt{N}D<-t\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})<\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-\frac{te^{-x}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})>\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})+\frac{te^{-x}}{\sqrt{N}}\right).\end{split}

Then

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})<\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})-\frac{te^{-x}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})<x-te^{-x}N^{-1/2}+Ce^{-2x}N^{-1}\right),\\
&\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})>\mathbb{E}g(\hat{p}^{\prime})+\frac{te^{-x}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})>x+te^{-x}N^{-1/2}-Ce^{-2x}N^{-1}\right).\end{split}

We first investigate the lower tail bound:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(g(\hat{p}^{\prime})<x-te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}+Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}^{\prime}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}-Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{2N}\right)\end{split}

When
t>1
, since
te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}-Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}>\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}
by the assumption on
N
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}-Ce^{2|x|}N^{-1}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{2N}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{2N}\right).\end{split}

When
t>6e^{x}N^{1/2}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{2N}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}<\frac{1.1}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}\leq\frac{1.1}{\exp(-x+3)+1}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}\leq\frac{1.1}{e^{3}\exp(-x)+1}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{2(\exp(-x)+1)}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(p^{\prime}\leq\frac{p}{2}\right)=0,\end{split}

where the last identity comes from the definition of
p^{\prime}
. When
1<t<6e^{x}N^{1/2}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{2N}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<\frac{1+1/N}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}-\frac{1}{1+e^{-x}}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<\frac{e^{-x}\left(1-e^{\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}}\right)+N^{-1}(1+e^{-x})}{\left(\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1\right)(1+e^{-x})}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<\frac{-\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}}-1\right)+N^{-1}}{\exp\left(-x+\frac{1}{2}te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}\right)+1}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<-\frac{1}{4}\frac{te^{|x|}N^{-1/2}}{\exp\left(-x+3\right)+1}+\frac{1}{N(e^{-x}+1)}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<-\frac{1}{4e^{3}}tN^{-1/2}+\frac{1}{N^{-1/2}(e^{-x}+1)}N^{-1/2}\right)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{p}-p<-\frac{1}{4e^{3}}tN^{-1/2}+ctN^{-1/2}\right)\leq\exp\left(-ct^{2}\right).\end{split}

Thus we have proved that
\forall t>1
, there exists constant
c
, such that the lower tail bound

\mathbb{P}\left(e^{x}\sqrt{N}D<-t\right)\leq e^{-ct^{2}}.

We can prove the similar result for upper tail bound. Thus,
\forall p\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}|e^{x}\sqrt{N}D|^{q}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(|e^{x}\sqrt{N}D|>t\right)qt^{q-1}dt\leq\int_{0}^{1}qt^{q-1}dt+\int_{1}^{\infty}e^{-ct^{2}}qt^{q-1}dt\\
\leq&\int_{0}^{1}qt^{q-1}dt+\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-ct^{2}}qt^{q-1}dt=1+c^{-\frac{q}{2}}q\Gamma\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)\\
\leq&1+qc^{-\frac{q}{2}}\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}\leq\left(1+q^{\frac{1}{q}}c^{-1/2}\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)^{q},\end{split}

where we use
x^{q}+y^{q}\leq(x+y)^{q}
for all
x,y\geq 0,q\geq 1
. Then it follows that

\sup_{q\geq 1}q^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left|e^{x}\sqrt{N}D\right|^{q}\right)^{1/q}\leq q^{-1/2}\left(1+q^{\frac{1}{q}}c^{-1/2}\left(\frac{q}{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)\leq(1+\frac{e^{1/e}c^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{2}})=:K_{0},

and we have proved the second inequality of (
E.16
).
\square
